<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">PRESENTATION</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Operator</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rich Dietz&#160;
&#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">SBC Communications - VP IR</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Good afternoon, everyone, and thanks for
joining us here today. I&#146;m Rich Dietz, Vice President of Investor Relations for
SBC. On behalf of everyone here both at SBC and AT&amp;T, welcome. We are
really thrilled to be here, and we really appreciate your time and attention
this afternoon.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Yesterday, we announced our agreement to acquire
AT&amp;T. We followed that with a conference call. We are here today to follow
up, provide you some additional background, and to give you a chance to hear in
person from the key executives, those who will be executing on this deal, this
exciting new platform that the combined Company will be able to create.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Today we have Ed Whitacre, SBC&#146;s Chairman
and CEO, Dave Dorman, Chairman and CEO of AT&amp;T; both will have opening
comments. Then you will hear from Randall Stephenson, SBC&#146;s Chief Operating
Officer, along with 2 key operational personnel and executives of SBC.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">First, John Stankey, Senior Executive Vice
President and Chief Technology Officer. John is responsible for a number of the
areas including network planning, IT, enterprise product platforms, data
services, sales operation support, and procurement. After John you will hear
from Mark Keiffer, SBC&#146;s Senior Vice President for Business Marketing. After
that Rick Lindner, SBC&#146;s CFO will provide the financial wrapup and a summary of
our deal. Then, finally, of course we will conclude with Qs and As.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Also with us today for the Q&amp;A session
are several additional key executives including Bill Hannigan, AT&amp;T&#146;s
President and Chief Operating Officer, and Tom Horton, AT&amp;T&#146;s Vice Chairman
and Financial Officer.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Before we get started, for those who are
taking part via the Web or by phone today, let me remind you that the
presentation slides for this event are available on the investor relations page
of SBC&#146;s website, SBC.com.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Also I want to mention a word about the
Q&amp;A session. We will hold all the questions until the speakers are finished
and have a full Q&amp;A session; and at all times the speakers and other
executives will be available on stage for that Q&amp;A session. Today&#146;s meeting
is for analysts, so ask that the analysts are the ones who ask the questions
during the Q&amp;A session. To help those who are listening </font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<div style="font-family:Times New Roman;">

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">via the Web, we ask that you identify
yourself and the firm that you&#146;re representing.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Of course I need to cover our Safe Harbor
statement. Information set forth in this presentation contains financial
estimates and other forward-looking statements that are subject to risks and
uncertainties, and actual results might differ materially. Information about
the factors that may affect future results are contained in the slides
accompanying this speech. They have been posted to SBC&#146;s website and are
contained in SBC&#146;s filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission, which
are available also at SBC&#146;s website, wwwdSEC.gov.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">SBC disclaims any obligation to update and
revise statements contained in this presentation based on new information or
otherwise.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This presentation may contain certain
non-GAAP financial measures. Reconciliations between the non-GAAP financial
measures and the GAAP financial measures are available on SBC&#146;s website,
SBC.com investor relations. I also want to call your attention to the
information regarding SEC filings that can be found on slide 5 in your </font></b><font color="black" style="color:black;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">booklet.</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:black;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Okay. At this point, with no further
delay, it is my great honor to introduce Ed Whitacre, SBC&#146;s CEO. Ed?</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:black;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Ed Whitacre&#160;
&#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">SBC Communications - Chairman &amp; CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:black;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:black;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Thank
you, Rich, and good afternoon to all of you. On behalf</font></b><font color="black" style="color:windowtext;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;"> of everyone at SBC and AT&amp;T, let me start by saying
how excited we are to be here today to present you the details of a transaction
that we believe will truly transform our business.</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Our agreement to acquire AT&amp;T is a
positive step forward for SBC and our stockholders. We are creating the
prototype communications company for the 21st century, opening a new era of
innovation and competition for customers everywhere. If you know me, and some
of you do, you know I would not say that lightly.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Over the years we have been involved in a
lot of deals at SBC. We have done mergers that carried a bigger price tag than
this one. We have made acquisitions that gave us a larger customer base than
this one. We have done those which doubled our employee count. But we have
never made an acquisition that was more important to our Company&#146;s future, to
our nation&#146;s role in the global communications marketplace.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The combination of SBC and AT&amp;T puts
together a terrific set of network assets, assets that complement one another
very well. It adds product sets and service capabilities that will speed our
development in key areas. As a result, the new Company can deliver the advanced
data and IP-based services that are the future of communications, and do it
much faster and more efficiently than either Company could have done it alone.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It accelerates our expansion in the
enterprise space. It gives us nationwide presence and global reach. All this
comes with huge synergy opportunities that start right after closing and ramp
fast. We have said that we expect to gain approvals and close the transaction
early next year. I expect us to beat that.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Today you will hear from operating
executives responsible for delivering on the great opportunities we have. We
are leaving a lot of time for questions. We will spend as much time as you want
answering your questions today.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This transaction is about change. Big
changes are taking place in our industry, changes that are forcing companies to
confront their future. We are leading that change; we are not waiting for it to
happen to us. This is a great combination. Customers will benefit. It will
create value for stockholders, and the Company we are creating through this
transaction and through our Cingular Wireless deal last year will be built for
tremendous success in the years ahead.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It is the right products, it is the right
networks, it is the right cost structure at the right time and at the right
price. We have a great story to tell about the terrific opportunities ahead for
our stockholders, employees, and customers. Again I want to thank you for
joining us today. At this point I will ask Dave Dorman to add a couple of
comments. Dave?</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">&#160;Dave Dorman&#160; &#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">AT&amp;T - Chairman &amp;
CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Thank you, Ed. I would like to echo Ed&#146;s
enthusiasm for this transaction. We are truly very excited about the
opportunity to combine forces with SBC, and we do believe we are creating the
industry&#146;s premier communications and networking company with this transaction.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This deal allows AT&amp;T to continue to
play a significant role in building the defining entity for telecom in the 21st
century. By combining with SBC now we create a Company capable of delivering
advanced network technologies and a full suite of integrated communications
services throughout America and around the world.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I&#146;m asked the question, why did AT&amp;T
agree to this merger, and why now? Clearly, we have moved through a period of
prolonged challenge and instability in the telecom industry in the recent few
years. We have had dynamic and dramatic advances in technology. We have had an
ever-shifting regulatory environment. We have had intense and increased
competition; and we have had fraud on the part of several of our competitors.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<div>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The combined impact of these factors has
forever altered the telecom landscape and forced us to make more than our share
of strategic shifts. Most recently, regulatory reversals led us to redefine our
business model, stepping back from actively marketing traditional consumer
services to focus our full energies on the enterprise market and the emerging
technologies and product sets that we refer to as SoIP, or services over IP.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Given the continued challenges and
competitive forces we&#146;re sure to face, we understand that operational scale and
scope will be critically important in defining the winners and losers in this
industry over the next several years and in fact the next decade.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">In recent months, AT&amp;T has sought to
insure our long-term viability by building on our existing technology and scale
advantages, as well as our significant financial strength and our global reach.
We have made great progress in positioning this Company for the long-term and
winning the opportunity to fight on and compete for the future at a time when
many had written us off. I&#146;m proud of that.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Through this deal, we now have the
opportunity to accelerate our growth and transformation by combining our
operational and financial advantages with those of SBC. SBC has a growing and
significant consumer and small-business presence, which we don&#146;t; a
demonstrated commitment to broadband deployment, which we don&#146;t; a vibrant
local and regional market set for voice data, DSL, wireless and the new
frontier of video, which we don&#146;t.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So why now? The fact is both of us are at
a point where we&#146;re fundamentally redefining ourselves, in our case moving away
from traditional consumer services and towards a deeper penetration in the
enterprise market, and VoIP and Wireless offerings through others and our own
efforts. SBC in contrast is not a major player in the enterprise space and is
instead focused on expanding their current suite of bundled service offerings
to consumers and small business while beginning to play at the enterprise.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Also, from a regulatory perspective, you
could not expect me not to get a chance to say it is no longer unthinkable.
Going forward, this combination will allow us to continue to providing
high-quality services to our customers, which they have comes to rely on, and
while assuring, as Ed said earlier, that a U.S.-based firm will serve as a
global leader in networking and telecommunications.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It provides AT&amp;T with additional
financial strength and stability to accelerate the innovating and investing we
have been doing to change our networks and to change our business processes.
AT&amp;T brings to SBC more than just its global network, services customers,
into a combined company. It also brings the people of AT&amp;T and their unique
skills and experience in competing and serving customers on a national and global
basis, particularly in the business market.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I know that Ed Whitacre has enormous
respect for the history and significance of AT&amp;T as a corporate icon, and
he has welcomed AT&amp;T&#146;s people with a great statement yesterday &#151; welcome
home. I know that he fully values their knowledge, their expertise, and the
excellence that we bring to the table and they bring to the table. And that is
a big part of the reason I believe Ed pursued this deal, taking this step which
is a bold one, to reinvent this industry.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">While it is certainly ironic, and many
people will write and opine in the coming days, that a Baby Bell &#151; and SBC is
no baby &#151; is acquiring its former parent some 20 years after the divestiture.
It is the right move at the right time for both Companies, and is an exciting
opportunity to reshape the future of the industry in a positive direction.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We are committing to delivering the
potential synergies and efficiencies of the combined Company, and we&#146;re
confident that they are achievable. The fact is this combination is good for
our shareowners, it is good for our customers, and it&#146;s good for employees who
now have the opportunity to play a role in building that defining entity of
telecom for the 21st century.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">With that, let me turn it back over to Ed
&#151; or Randall. Sorry.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">&#160;Randall Stephenson&#160; &#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">SBC Communications - COO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Thanks, Dave. It&#146;s good to be here this
afternoon. You heard from me at length yesterday, so I am going to be brief
here this afternoon. What I want to do is put before you a couple of our operating
guys who worked extensively with Bill Hannigan and his organization in AT&amp;T
to pull together the data that gave us the conference to do this transaction,
and that we can pull these synergies together that we have talked about.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I want to accomplish just a couple of
things. The first is just give you a little bit of an appreciation for the kind
of company we have created your here by putting these 2 companies together.
Second, I want you to walk out of here with a real strong appreciation and
comfort level for what it will take to bring these synergies to pass.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">When you step back from this, and you have
heard Dave and Ed both talk about this, the set of assets, network assets that
we&#146;re bringing together is impressive. It is very impressive. You start with
AT&amp;T, they have a &#151; it is truly a state-of-the-art IP backbone that as big
as just about any backbone in the world. It&#146;s got incredible capacity on it.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It&#146;s got the capacity to more than
accommodate what SBC will put on this network, and we will be putting a lot on
it. We have a lot of broadband customers, business customers, and we have
Lightspeed, Project Lightspeed coming, where we will begin deploying IP video
and carrying video over the IP backbone. So having this kind of capacity is
very, very important to us.</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<div>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Add to that the national network that they
have. This is a state-of-the-art national long distance network. This thing has
MPLS at the core; it has it at the edge; it is as good as you are going to find
in the United States. Then you extend from that national network a global
presence and global network assets, and you have a network that we just think
is second to none. This is a hard to duplicate network.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">But now if you step back and you combine
to that SBC&#146;s network assets, which are also very, very impressive, and you
start with our local network, we have a very, very dense local network that
covers one-third of the United States. If you think about this thing, it goes
from New Haven, Connecticut, to Chicago to Dallas to San Francisco and L.A.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">On top of that local dense network, we
have a broadband network that is covering 80 percent of that network. On top of
that we have 5.1 million broadband customers, one of the largest broadband
customer bases in the United States, growing faster than anybody else&#146;s
broadband customer base in the United States.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Now combine that with the largest and I
would say densest, most spectrum rich wireless network in the United States
that is soon-to-be fully IP capable; and you put those together, and you have
what I believe is a very, very impressive set of assets.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Bring to that what AT&amp;T is bringing to
this Company, and that is a product set that is really impressive. When you
talk about their IP product set, a consumer Voice over IP product &#151; we&#146;re
working hard to develop something like this. It&#146;s done. They have a VoIP
product set. IP VPN; unified communications. A very, very rich product set that
we believe we can leverage very quickly.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">If you look at just the functionality, I
would tell you one of the things that impressed us the most when we looked at
this organization is what this Company has done in terms of streamlining its
operations on the high-end business side. Everything from ordering to
provisioning to billing &#151; it is something you&#146;re not going to find in another
asset in the United States.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So if you can take those critical network
assets, the functionality and the product set we have talked about here, and
you integrate those two, what you get are synergies that are quite frankly
unlike anything that I have seen before.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We have done a lot of transactions as you
know over the past, and we have done transactions where we combined
Southwestern Bell and Pacific Bell. If you think about that transaction, the
synergies came from overheads and IT procurement costs and then best practices
between the two companies. We did the same exact thing with Ameritech. We have
not done a large-scale transaction like this, where there is such an overlap of
capability, where we have redundancy in some of our network assets; and as a
result what we see are synergies that are quite frankly unlike anything that we
have done or anything we have experienced or seen before.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">If you add it up, what John Stankey and
Mark Keiffer are going to show you the details of is a transaction that yields
$15 billion in synergies, about the same value as the transaction itself. The
great part about these synergies &#151; again because of the redundancy of the cost
structures and such &#151; is that these synergies begin immediately.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This is not something that will take 3
years of IT development to create. We can begin to realize these synergies very
quickly. In fact if this transaction closes in 2006, by the year 2008 we think
we can take our run rate cost down by about $2 billion a year, and that that
will ramp to somewhere around $3 billion by 2011. So it&#146;s a very exciting
opportunity.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">If $2 billion seems daunting to you, I
said this yesterday and I will repeat it. Consider that in our area we&#146;re
trying to ramp in the enterprise space. We have just begun and we&#146;re trying to
create scale. We are spending in sales, network, operating support, customer
care, somewhere at about a $1.6 billion run rate today. So am I optimistic we
can get 2 billion? Yes. You throw in the procurement opportunity, IT, the
corporate overheads, I think a $2 billion a year run rate savings is very, very
achievable.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">If you could just for a moment consider
fast forwarding this tape about 3 years, and this integration has been
completed, the approvals are done, and we have a company that I truly believe
in 3 years has a cost lecture that is best in class. When you are computing in
the environment of telecom, which is a highly competitive industry, having a
cost structure that is best in class is very, very critical. It&#146;s a good
position to be in, and it&#146;s an advantageous position to be in.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">You add to that a service set that we have
talked about already, we think you have a Company that fundamentally changes
the U.S. telecom position, and it changes SBC and America &#151; AT&amp;T&#146;s position
as well. So we&#146;re very excited about this. We&#146;re very anxious to get this
transaction moving. What I want to do is introduce Mark Keiffer to you, and he
is going to talk to you a little bit more about the marketing and sales side of
this.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">&#160;Mark Keiffer&#160; &#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">SBC Communications - SVP
Business Marketing</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Good afternoon. I think I have the opportunity
to talk with you about probably the most exciting thing that is going on. Bill
Hannigan and his team, along with Ray Wilkins, Bob Ferguson, and several people
at SBC have had a lot of opportunity to talk with our customers since the deal
has been announced.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I want to take just a minute to step back
and share with you both, from a customer perspective and what our perception
has been, about what the key things that we&#146;re going to be able to deliver, </font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<div>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">again, from a customer perspective going
forward. After all, they are the ones that pay our bill, and we have got to
make sure that as we work through this process we&#146;re going to deliver the
things that they need in order for their business to be successful going
forward.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It really boils down to 3 things. What we
have heard is reach, solution set, and people. What I am going to do is quickly
walk you through how that comes together. Kind of tying things that Randall
alluded to, as he went through his presentation, and the things that Dave and
Ed talked about, what that means to customer and what we are going to be able
to do with that in the marketplace.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">From a reach perspective, this ties to
what Randall was talking about on a dense network from an SBC perspective. The
local network, not surprisingly when you look at the Southwest, the Midwest and
the West, SBC brings to bear a density of our network that allows us to deliver
on a regional perspective wherever our customers&#146; footprint needs us to go.
Again from a customer&#146;s perspective, delivering to their footprint.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Now what you see on that map is the East
Coast looks a little barren, doesn&#146;t it ? We have been working to build that
out but we&#146;re not there yet.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Let me show you the next map. This is a
map of AT&amp;T&#146;s POPs. What do you see along the Eastern corridor there? A lot
of density. So take the assets that SBC has, combine those with the assets that
AT&amp;T brings to bear, from a national perspective we will be able to
deliver, from a customer&#146;s perspective their locations, where their branch
offices are, what they need to be successful going forward.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Now lay on top of that the global network.
At the high-end when a customer is global we will be able to leverage the
national assets with that global network for their footprint. In the
discussions that we have had with our customers this is one of the biggest
things they&#146;re excited about, because no matter where their location is &#151; from
the A location to the Z location &#151; we&#146;re going to be able to provide the
products and services that they need to be successful. So reach, a very key
element.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The second piece that comes to bear is
solution set. If we take a look at the products and services that SBC brings,
with the products and services that AT&amp;T brings to the table from their
perspective, what you see is a very broad and deep portfolio. But that is kind
of a broad and deep portfolio of products. What customers are so excited about
when they look at this is now the solutions that we will be able to deliver
will be tailored to where they are in the business segment.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This is a real key point. We as a combined
entity will be able to take a customer wherever they are in the technology
continuum, and take them to where they need to be to enable their success.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So what does that mean? There are
customers that will be on a voice network for quite awhile. They have made some
investments; they have not depreciated those totally; they have got to stay
with those assets until they depreciate. They may not be ready yet. That is
fine.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We will be able to work with them to
deliver solution sets that will enable them to be successful. They want to move
up market, data perhaps; we will have the solution sets for that. The key is,
wherever they are in the continuum we will be able to deliver a solution that
is going to allow them to be successful from their definition of success.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The other thing that is really neat about
this when you are bringing these assets together &#151; Randall alluded to this &#151; is
the wireless piece. Not only will we be able to deliver this kind of the definition
of technology today, but when you look at the assets as they come together &#151; sometimes
I think even I get confuse on this sometimes &#151; but really it is about optics
and the edge of network.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">When these assets come together, we will
have the ability &#151; no matter wherever technology goes, and whatever the
applications may be &#151; we will be able to deliver against that.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I started with Southwestern Bell in 1982
as an engineer. The edge of the network when I started in this business was 4
walls and a central office for a switch. That was the edge of the network. Over
time what we have seen happen is the edge continued to get more blurred and
more blurred and more blurred.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">At some point in time, this will become
the edge of the network. Wherever you are, the device that you are carrying,
that will be the edge of the network. We will be able to take that product
suite and wrap managed services around it for those who want us to, and be able
to manage that for them.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">But again a competitive lever that we will
have &#151; if customers want to manage that themselves that will absolutely be able
to. If they want us to take care of it for them, we will be able to do you
that. That flexibility has come back loud and clear to us as one of the key
things that customers are excited about in bringing these Companies together.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So reach and solution set were two. The
third one I talked about was people. Certainly if you look at things like
certifications, formal training, things like that, from an SBC and an AT&amp;T
perspective we bring best in class human assets to the table when we talk about
delivering those kinds of things.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">But the other thing that we bring, and
this came across loud and clear with some of the customers I was talking about
yesterday, is that we can talk about &#151; we can give examples of successes that
we have had, and how we can leverage those successes in that company&#146;s
business. It is not a trial. It is not a first time out of the </font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">box try for us. So we will bring a history
of performance and able to deliver in the marketplace, something that we&#146;re
finding to be very, very important as we move forward.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">This last point has to do specifically
with the accounting (ph). We are going to start very briefly talk about some of
the synergies. When you look at these numbers, and I am just going to talk
about the sales marketing and sales operation numbers, if you look at that you
see a 17 percent reduction and a 14 percent reduction. You might say, well,
those are fairly conservative, Mark. And from just a pure numbers perspective
those would be.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">But one of the key things for us is the
account relationship that we have with our customers. As we go through the next
year, getting this deal done, after the deal is closed, we are not going to
allow ourselves to forget how important the relationship that we have with our
customers is. That is absolutely critical.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So we will be very careful as we move
through the synergies that we have from the sales force perspective &#151; because
there are obviously overlaps when we bring this together &#151; we will be very
careful to make sure that we do nothing to jeopardize the position that we have
with our customer base.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So when you kind of step back from this
and look at it from a customers&#146; perspective, loud and clear, the reach &#151; absolutely
love it. The solution set that that we&#146;re going to be able to bring to the
market, it is something that will enable them to be successful as they define
it. And we will absolutely have the people, best in class people to deliver
that solution as we go forward.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We have got a lot of synergies and
opportunities that we&#146;re going to be talking about. At this time I want to ask
John Stankey to come up, and he will walk us through those.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">&#160;John Stankey&#160; &#151; <i><font style="font-style:italic;">SBC Communications - SEVP
&amp; CTO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Good afternoon. Nice to be with all of you
today. They don&#146;t let me out much, but they promised me a free lunch for coming
up here, so I got out from my office and agreed to come. Before I jump into kind
of the meat of what I would like to discuss today, I would like to just offer
some observations. Some of these reinforce what Randall had to say, but a few
of them I think give you kind of an operating perspective on additional things
that we think might bring to the future benefits of this great combination.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Randall talked about the dense local
broadband access that SBC has and marrying that with the IP infrastructure and
the backbone that AT&amp;T has. I would also say that there is an operational
opportunity there in terms of SBS is real strong on Layer 1 transport and how
to operate those networks on a local basis. Maybe the best in the country when
you look at operational metrics. Marry that with what I&#146;m about to show you,
which is clearly by far and away the best entity in managing Layer 2 and 3
networks, which is the AT&amp;T Company.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I would also point out that we have great
opportunities when we think about things like security. AT&amp;T has done a
tremendous amount of work in their labs on security offerings and how to manage
these new IP networks that are evolving, that is really frame breaking in a lot
of ways. SBC has market reach down to the lower segments of the market that
AT&amp;T normally could not get at, that are going to be in need of these kind
of solutions and an opportunity to spread this.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">IP is a wonderful technology as you think
about moving that out; but it also brings with it a tremendous amount of
complexity and new intellectual property that is necessary to effectively
manage it. I think there is great opportunity there.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">When I think about combining the
technology base from a software and IT practice perspective, SBC is strong in
care and billing operations; and I look at AT&amp;T and have been incredibly
impressed with what they can do around the network elements, and what they do
with network element configuration and the marriage of those two strength
together.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The growth in IP traffic that we are
generating at the local level, they can help fill the backbone. I also think
about the work we have begun to do inside of SBC around, how do we take costs
out of our rural and less dense operating areas? We have been looking at
wireless local loop possibilities as a way to do that, and how to use some of
our spectrum position with Cingular to possibly change that paradigm. AT&amp;T
brings out of the lab some fabulous research that we believe will help jump
start that paradigm, and look for an opportunity for us to alter cost structure
in other parts of our local access business.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We have a world-class procurement
operation inside SBC, and now we get to be unleashed on a new spend over at
AT&amp;T that we believe will net some benefit; and I will talk a little bit
about that as well.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">And then spent a lot of time as we were
going through this looking at issues like switched access, special access,
rates, and pricing, and what is going to go on there. I don&#146;t know where you
come out on that, but after I have fully examined it what I have concluded is
it is kind of hard to predict the future. But what I do see is a tremendous
hedge on some major shifts that are going to occur in regulation over time as
we look at carrier access reform, for example. This allows this combined
Company to really benefit on all sides of the equation as that comes out.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So with that, let me kind of jump in to
some of the key points. First of all let me talk to you about 4 execution
imperatives. The first one is going to be operational cost reduction. As
Randall indicated to you earlier, this is not a best practices exercise. This
is a forklift of how AT&amp;T runs their enterprise business segment, and using
</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>
