<div>

<p align="right" style="font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Also, yesterday SBC management glowingly
almost bragged that the shares being offered are less than the actual net
present value of the synergies. Given that you have $10 billion of revenue
coming off, there must be some residual value that should have been included in
the consideration offer to AT&amp;T shareholders.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Finally, I could not care less that 3
people are going to go from the Board of AT&amp;T to the Board of SBC, given
the presiding over a relative industrial decline that that enterprise has
witnessed over the last 25 years. So if you could (technical difficulty)
comment.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Dave Dorman <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - Chairman &amp; CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;In
terms of the Board of AT&amp;T, I think there are 3 &#151; 4 Board members of the 9
outside directors who joined shortly before or shortly after I became CEO. So 6
of the 10 did not preside over the industrial decline or whatever phrase you
talked about. They came in afterwards.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">As far as the directors going on the SBC
Board, I cannot speak for Ed, but SBC&#146;s Board is a very experienced Board. On
average the age of SBC&#146;s Board members is older than AT&amp;T&#146;s Board members.
I think it is opportunity for SBC to pick up with their consent a couple of
great directors, in addition to myself.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">As far as my role is concerned it is very
straightforward. That is up to SBC, Ed, and the Board of Directors. I have
offered my services to be there as long as they would like me to in an
appropriate role. So there is no soft promise or hard promise or anything else.
My intent is to make this successful and deliver value for shareholders, and I
will participate in it as long as I&#146;m welcome to participate.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rich Dietz <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
SBC Communications - VP IR</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Tom Watts <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Tom

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Just a second question. AT&amp;T has a

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rick Lindner <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
SBC Communications - SEVP &amp; CFO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Thanks. Let me talk just a minute about the
wireless question, and I will hand off to talk about the managed services question.
On the wireless side, in terms of what we have modeled, we have not modeled
additional synergies, and the numbers that you have seen today do not include
additional synergies from the wireless side.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Having said that, we think this
transaction brings additional value to Cingular. It gives us the opportunity to
jointly market the advanced data services and wireless into the largest
businesses in the United States with 2 footprints, both on the wireline, the
data, the advanced services side, and the wireless side that are very
complementary.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So I think there is a tremendous
opportunity there. It is an upside to any of the numbers that we have shown you

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Bill Hannigan <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">There are Cingular minutes of use that are
included in some of those synergies that I talked about. It is one of the
fastest growing components of our wireless traffic, and it is all the more
reason why getting to a good cost structure on that is really exciting to us as
well. Because as those wireless minutes continue to grow we get a really robust
architecture to deliver them on.</font></b></p>

Just looking at the synergy assumptions; you said there&#146;s about 2.5 billion or

7,700 layoffs, gets you to around 200,000 (technical difficulty) Seems a little
high. I was wondering if there are other redundancies that have yet to be

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;I
think first with respect to the employee numbers, I think your math is off
there. If you go through the presentation slides, start adding up those

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I am sorry, your second question was? With
respect to the unions, we have strong relationships as you know with the CWA.
AT&amp;T has strong relationships with the CWA. As we move forward on these
initiatives &#151; and I should mention both Companies have efficiency and workforce
reduction plans in place that we have announced on a stand-alone basis,
relative to 2005. All the things we&#146;re talking about today obviously are
incremental to that and in essence a continuation of it post-closing.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We have the ability within those
agreements to manage force, and rules in place to do that. We will obviously
follow that. We will work with the union on that. But in addition, if you just
look at the AT&amp;T portion of the employee base, their workforce from a union
CWA standpoint is much smaller.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Dave Dorman <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - Chairman &amp; CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;I
think ended the year at AT&amp;T with about 15,000 represented employees
between the CWA and IBEA. Our current plans including announced center closings
that we had in the last 2 weeks would see that number decline well below 10,000
by year-end.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So if you look at AT&amp;T&#146;s total
workforce, we&#146;re talking about less than 10,000 union employees, with some
significant percentage of them being call center employees in the declining
consumer business, which undoubtedly could be combined with SBC on a
productivity and efficiency basis. So the union from our perspective is pretty
small part of our overall base just as our business has changed.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Unidentified
Company Representative</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;We
have been able to effectively manage force without doing significant layoffs
over the last couple of years. Our attrition rates are really fairly high,
particularly when you look at some of the areas we&#146;re talking about downsizing
here, that will attrite somewhere around 1,000 employees of month. So we think
if we are very aggressive and manage our force, we can do this without dramatic
layoffs.</font></b></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rich Dietz <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rick Klugman <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Rick Klugman, Jefferies. You present

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">Second question if I could, you talked

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rick Lindner <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
SBC Communications - SEVP &amp; CFO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;I
think with respect how we have modeled and looked at the business, clearly we
have taken into account the fact that within AT&amp;T there are several things
happening and several moving parts.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">One, as you heard Dave talk about, is they
have pulled back and are declining revenues fairly rapidly in their consumer
business. There are portions of their business revenue stream that are voice
related or LD voice related; and those have been declining. Or in many cases
what is happening is you see customers moving to new technologies, and that is
causing some of the revenue decline.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">At the same time, I hope what you got the
impression of today and a good feel for today is, as we move traffic and
customers to new services and an IP-based broadbased network, that network also
carries with it a much lower operating costs. In doing that, what AT&amp;T has
been successful in is, as revenues have been declining, their cost side has
been declining also.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So that is how we have looked at the
business. Frankly it is one of the things that makes this transaction a little
more difficult to analyze. You have to get beyond that.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">We do believe what comes out the other
side of this though, with the combination of assets we have talked about, is a
very strong Company particularly when you are looking at the enterprise </font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="#003399" face="Times New Roman"><img width="575" height="51" src="form425image032.jpg"></font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="#003399" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<div>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="#003399" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:right;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:black;font-size:10.0pt;">FINAL TRANSCRIPT</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;">SBC - SBC Communications and AT&amp;T Analyst Meeting Webcast</font></b></p>

<p align="center" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">space. In the enterprise space there is
going to be and is demand there for future services, integrated voice and data
networks, managed services, all of the things that this Company is going to be
able to provide.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">When you break down the revenues, it is
true there are some categories that are declining rapidly. There also
categories that are increasing at double-digit rates. As you look ahead, there&#146;s
certainly next year AT&amp;T has already provided guidance that indicates a
substantial decline next year, double-digit decline. But as you get out a
couple of years, our belief is, as that transition slows, the revenues begin to
stabilize as well and the margins stabilize.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">I think you talked about the material
adverse change clause; and that would be really based off of the kinds of
projections and the outlook that they have put forward as the base case.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Dave Dorman <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - Chairman &amp; CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Could I add one thing to that? When you talk
about valuing this business, and Rick talked about the declining revenue
streams where they&#146;re leaving the business, the business assets that they have
created &#151; there is not another business like that. There is not another set of
assets like this available. I would suggest to you worldwide you could not find
another side of assets like this. So you are getting a very, very unique set of
assets.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">But there is a second piece to that when
you look at it from SBC&#146;s side. That is, these large fixed cost businesses, you
don&#146;t get many shots at changing your cost structure like we&#146;ve got a shot at
changing ours with this one. This will fundamentally change the cost structure
of our business, and that is as big a driver to this transaction from our side
as anything else we can come up with.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">It changes the way you look at the whole
access regime and how you are exposed on the access revenue side that John
talked about. So this goes beyond buying a declining revenue stream. There is a
great set of core assets; and at the end of the day we will be able to
fundamentally change our cost structure and be in a class we think that is best
in class ahead of anybody else. Bill, do you want to talk about the (technical
difficulty) ?</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Bill Hannigan <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - President &amp; COO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;I
was just going to comment on Rick&#146;s comment on the mix of our revenue stream by
segment, that he talked about earlier, 30 billion in revenue in 2004. 10
billion of that is mass market. So think about the 85 percent of our business
portfolio that is enterprise, global, and government, more than $20 billion.
The rate of decline is, we anticipate, single digit.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So there is still plenty of price pressure
in the marketplace, but we&#146;re holding or taking share in that 85 percent of the
revenue stream that is in the business portfolio of AT&amp;T.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Rich Dietz <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
SBC Communications - VP IR</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;We
have time for one last question. In the back here.</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Oliver Boone <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
Alliance Capital - Analyst</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Oliver Boone (ph) from Alliance Capital. Two
questions, the first is on the SBC side. At closing, will you guarantee the
remaining AT&amp;T bonds that are outstanding?</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">The second related question for Mr. Dorman
is, if you could just remind us what the remaining part of the debt retirement
program is? I know there are a couple of maturities between here and there, but
what is sort of remaining outstanding in terms of going back to the previous
question on excess free cash flow?</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Dave Dorman <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - Chairman &amp; CEO</font></i></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;The
first part of your question related to the debt, we are reviewing the AT&amp;T
outstanding debt and the covenants associated with it. We have not made final
determinations on guarantees. But I will tell you that many of those debt
issuances include some rate adjustment that is based on the ratings.</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">So certainly from our perspective, once
the transaction is closed, there may be some advantages to guaranteeing that
debt, if we&#146;re able to reduce the interest expense, and we&#146;ll certainly factor
that into our thought process there. Tom, do you want to take the second part?</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0in 0in .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="border:none;font-weight:bold;line-height:normal;margin:4.0pt 0in;padding:0in;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#160;Tom Horton <i><font style="font-style:italic;">&#160;</font></i>&#151;<i><font style="font-style:italic;">
AT&amp;T - Vice Chairman &amp; Financial Officer</font></i></font></b></p>

<p align="left" style="color:#003399;font-weight:bold;margin:0in 0in .0001pt;text-align:left;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;font-variant:normal !important;font-weight:normal;">&#160;Sure, Rick. As you may recall, we had a $3
billion Board-approved debt buyback program last year. We still have a couple
hundred million authorized under that. And we&#146;re going to take a step a back
and decide what we want to do in terms of buying back debt in the context of
this transaction in &#145;05.</font></b></p>

solicitation of proxies from AT&amp;T shareholders in respect of the proposed
transaction. Information regarding SBC&#146;s directors and executive officers is
available in SBC&#146;s proxy statement for its 2004 annual meeting of stockholders,
dated March 11, 2004, and information regarding AT&amp;T Corp.&#146;s directors and
executive officers is available in AT&amp;T Corp.&#146;s proxy statement for its
2004 annual meeting of shareholders, dated March 25, 2004. Additional
information regarding the interests of such potential participants will be
included in the registration and proxy statement and the other relevant
documents filed with the SEC when they become available.</font></p>

intentions and other statements that are not historical facts.&#160; Such statements are based upon the current
beliefs and expectations of SBC&#146;s and AT&amp;T&#146;s management and are subject to
significant risks and uncertainties.&#160;
Actual results may differ from those set forth in the forward-looking
statements.</font></p>

transaction on the proposed terms and schedule; the failure of AT&amp;T
shareholders to approve the transaction; the risk that the businesses will not

synergies from the transaction may not be fully realized or may take longer to
realize than expected; disruption from the transaction making it more difficult
to maintain relationships with customers, employees or suppliers; competition
